\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{s_{k}}

defined in Section
5.2
, which are given by

(p,q)_{r}\mapsto\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{0}+\dots+p_{k}=p\\
q_{0}+\dots+q_{k}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\sum_{i}(d_{0}+\dots+d_{i-1})(d_{i}+r_{i})}(p_{0},q_{0})_{s_{0}}\otimes\dots\otimes(p_{k},q_{k})_{s_{k}}

Elements of
\mathrm{Inf}\big((M\circ N)\big)(n)
are linear combinations of formal composites

(p,q)_{s}\otimes(\nu_{0};\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{k})\sigma

where
s=\mid\nu_{0}\mid+\dots+\mid\nu_{k}\mid
,
\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
,
\nu_{0}\in M(k)
and
\nu_{1}\in N(i_{1}),\dots,\nu_{k}\in N(i_{k})
with
i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=n
. With this description we define the comparison map

\psi_{M,N}:\mathrm{Inf}(M\circ N)\to\mathrm{Inf}(M)\circ\mathrm{Inf}(N)

by letting

\psi_{M,N}(n)\big((p,q)_{s}\otimes(\nu_{0};\nu_{1},\dots,\nu_{k})\sigma\big):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{0}+\dots+p_{k}=p\\
q_{0}+\dots+q_{k}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon}(\nu_{0}^{p_{0},q_{0}};\nu_{1}^{p_{1},q_{1}},\dots,\nu_{k}^{p_{k},q_{k}})\sigma

where
\varepsilon=\sum_{i}(d_{0}+\dots+d_{i-1})(d_{i}+r_{i})
and
\nu_{r}^{p_{r},q_{r}}:=(p_{r},q_{r})_{d_{r}}\otimes\nu_{r}
and
d_{r}=|\nu_{r}|
. The map
\psi(n)
is obviously equivariant.

The fact that it is a map of bicomplexes and the required properties of
\psi_{M,N}
are now directly deduced from the symmetric monoidality of the inflation functor for the case of chain complexes of

\mathbf{k}
-modules, Proposition 4.1 in
[
20
]
.
Namely, the above diagram commutes and
\psi_{M,N}
is a weak equivalence.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

The inflation functor

\mathrm{Inf}\colon\mathbb{S}\,\text{-}\mathrm{Ch}^{\leq 0}\to\mathbb{S}\,\text{-}\mathrm{BiCo}^{\mathord{\mspace{1mu}\text{\hskip 1.50694pt\hbox to3.31pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.15pt\lower-0.15pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.17 0 L 4.17 4.17 L 0 4.17 M 2.29 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mspace{1mu}}}

sends quasi-isomorphisms to pluripotential weak equivalences.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\colon M\to N
be a quasi-isomorphism in
\mathbb{S}\,\text{-}\mathrm{Ch}^{\leq 0}
, i.e. a sequence of quasi-isomorphisms of cochain complexes
\varphi(n)\colon M(n)\to N(n)
compatible with the
\mathbb{S}_{n}
action. Then,

\mathrm{Inf}(\varphi)\colon\mathrm{Inf}(M)\to\mathrm{Inf}(N)

is a sequence of maps of bicomplexes

\mathrm{Inf}(\varphi)(n)\colon\mathrm{Inf}(M(n))\to\mathrm{Inf}(N(n))

compatible with the action of
\mathbb{S}_{n}
. By Proposition 3.9 in
[
20
]
, the functor
\mathrm{Inf}\colon\mathrm{Ch}^{\leq 0}\to\mathrm{BiCo}_{\mathbf{k}}^{\mathord{\mspace{1mu}\text{\hskip 1.50694pt\hbox to3.31pt{\vbox to3.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.15pt\lower-0.15pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@roundcap\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.17 0 L 4.17 4.17 L 0 4.17 M 2.29 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mspace{1mu}}}
sends quasi-isomorphisms to pluripotential weak equivalences and we obtain the desired result.
∎

Of course, the inflation functor can be extended in the same way to sequences

\mathcal{M}=\{\mathcal{M}(n)\}_{n\geq 0}

without the
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-action, so it may also be used in the corresponding
non-symmetric setting.

5.4.
Koszul dual cooperad

We next define the pluripotential Koszul dual cooperad of a quadratic operad
\mathcal{P}
in
\mathbf{k}
-vector spaces. Recall that, by definition, an
operadic quadratic data

(E,R)
in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbf{k}}
consists of an
\mathbb{S}
-module
E
and a sub-
\mathbb{S}
-module
R\subseteq\mathbb{T}(E)^{(2)}
,
E
then denotes the generating vector space and
R
the set of relations. A
quadratic operad
associated to the quadratic data
(E,R)
is the operad
\mathcal{P}(E,R):=\mathbb{T}(E)/(R)
.

For the remainder of the section,
\mathcal{P}
will denote a quadratic operad in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbf{k}}
, we will view this operad as an operad in

\mathrm{Ch}_{\mathbf{k}}
or in
\mathrm{BiCo}_{\mathbf{k}}
by placing it in degree 0 or bidegree
(0,0)
, with trivial differentials. We will denote by
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}=\mathcal{C}(sE,s^{2}R)
the dg Koszul dual cooperad of
\mathcal{P}
and by
\Delta
its cooperad structure (see Section 7.2 in
[
19
]
).

Following the classical case, one is led to define
the pluripotential Koszul dual cooperad of
\mathcal{P}
as

\mathcal{C}(\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}E,\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\text{-}2}R)
.
We will instead use the inflation functor, so that we can recycle the classical computations of Koszul dual cooperads.

Definition 5.13
.

Define the
pluripotential Koszul dual cooperad
of
\mathcal{P}
as

\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}:=\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})

with the cooperad structure given by

\Delta:\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Inf}(\Delta)}\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\xrightarrow{\psi_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}},\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}}}\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\circ\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}),

where
\psi
denotes the colax natural transformation of Proposition
5.11
.

Remark 5.14
.

Note that
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
can be viewed as a sub-cooperad of
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
, given by the elements of bidegrees
(*,0)
and, similarly, of bidegrees
(0,*)
.

Example 5.15
.

The pluripotential Koszul dual of
As
(as a non symmetric operad) is given by

As^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(n)=\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

Denote by

an element of arity
n
and bidegree
(p,q)
. Then, the cooperad structure is given by

\Delta(\mu_{n}^{p,q})=\sum_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{0}+\dots+p_{k}=p\\
q_{0}+\dots+q_{k}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\theta+\varepsilon}(\mu_{k}^{p_{0},q_{0}};\mu_{i_{1}}^{p_{1},q_{1}},\dots,\mu_{i_{k}}^{p_{k},q_{k}}),

where

and
\varepsilon=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(p_{0}+q_{0}+\dots+p_{j-1}+q_{j-1})(p_{j}+q_{j}+i_{j}-1)
. Therefore, the infinitesimal decomposition gives

\Delta_{(1)}(\mu_{n}^{p,q})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq i\leq k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{(i-1)(\ell+1)+\varepsilon}\mu_{k}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{i}\mu_{\ell}^{p_{2},q_{2}},

where
\varepsilon=(p_{1}+q_{1})(p_{2}+q_{2}+\ell+1).

For the symmetric version, we have

\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{1-n}\otimes\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{S}_{n}],

with the

action being trivial on
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}

.

Remark 5.16
.

Note that, since
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
is a sub-cooperad of the cofree cooperad, it is naturally weight graded. Then, the pluripotential Koszul dual
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
inherits a weight given by

\omega((p,q)_{n}\otimes\mu)=\omega(\mu)

where
\mu
is an element of
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
.

We will next define a twisting morphism

\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\to\mathcal{P}
.
Recall from Section
5.2
that
\mathfrak{s}_{1}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\to\mathbf{k}
is the map of bidegree
(1,0)
projecting
to
\mathbf{k}
. Likewise,
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\to\mathbf{k}
is the corresponding map of bidegree
(0,1)
.
We construct the following two equivariant maps:

\kappa^{10}\colon\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}=\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{C}(sE,s^{2}R))\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Inf}(sE)=\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}

and

\kappa^{01}\colon\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}=\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{C}(sE,s^{2}R))\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Inf}(sE)=\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\otimes E\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id}}E\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}(E,R).

Remark 5.17
.

Recall that there is a dg twisting morphism, which by abuse of notation we will still denote by
\kappa
,

\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}=\mathcal{C}(sE,s^{2}R)\twoheadrightarrow sE\rightarrow E\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}(E,R).

In fact,
\kappa^{10}=\mathfrak{s}\otimes\kappa
and
\kappa^{01}=\overline{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\kappa
, where
\mathfrak{s}(p,q)_{n}=\mathfrak{s}_{1}
if
n=1
and zero otherwise and similarly for
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}
.

Proposition 5.18
.

The pair
\kappa=(\kappa^{10},\kappa^{01})\colon\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\to\mathcal{P}
is a twisting morphism.

Proof.

A direct computation shows

[\partial,\kappa^{10}]=[{\bar{\partial}},\kappa^{01}]=[\partial,\kappa^{01}]+[{\bar{\partial}},\kappa^{10}]=0.

On the other hand,
\kappa
is only non-zero in weight 1. So the pre-Lie products
\kappa^{i,1-i}\star\kappa^{j,1-j}
, with
i,j\in\{0,1\}

are zero except maybe in weight 2. But in weight 2 we have

\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}(2)}_{pp}\to\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}(1)}_{pp}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}(1)}_{pp}\xrightarrow{\kappa^{**}\circ_{(1)}\kappa^{**}}(R)\rightarrow\mathbb{T}(E)^{(2)}/(R)=\mathcal{P}

which is the zero map.
∎

Recall that a dg-operad
\mathcal{P}
is said to be
Koszul
if and only if
its associated Koszul complex is acyclic, so there is a quasi-isomorphism

\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{I},

where
\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
is the Koszul complex of a quadratic dg operad (see Section 7.4 in
[
19
]
).
In particular, this definition depends on the chosen class of weak equivalences, which in the dg case are just quasi-isomorphisms. In the pluripotential case, we may consider the analogous definition with the corresponding weak equivalences.
To this end, we construct the twisted composite product
\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}
associated to the twisting morphism
\kappa:\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}
, see Section
5.1
for details. The following proposition shows that if
\mathcal{P}
is a Koszul operad in the dg sense, concentrated in degree 0, then it is also Koszul in the pluripotential sense.

Proposition 5.19
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a quadratic operad in
\mathbf{k}
-vector spaces that is Koszul as a dg-operad.
Then, there is a pluripotential weak equivalence

\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{I}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.12
, the inflation functor preserves weak equivalences.
Since
\mathcal{P}
is Koszul as a dg-operad, we have a pluripotential weak equivalence

\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{I}.

We define a map of bicomplexes
\varphi:\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}

using the
colax structure of the inflation functor given in Proposition
5.11

\varphi:=\psi_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}}:\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})=\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}

This is a linear map of bigraded vector spaces, and it suffices to see that it is compatible with the twisting differentials.
Note that
\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P})=\mathcal{P}
since it is concentrated in degree 0.

Since
\varphi
is constructed using the colax monoidal structure of
\mathrm{Inf}
, it is clear that, when we forget the twisting differentials, we have a map of bicomplexes

\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})

which is a pluripotential weak equivalence by Proposition
5.11
.
Denote by
d_{\kappa}
and
\overline{d}_{\kappa}
the twisting differentials

d_{\kappa},\overline{d}_{\kappa}:\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})\to\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})

of bidegree
(1,0)
and of bidegree
(0,1)
, respectively, obtained after inflation of the Koszul complex. Explicitly, these are given by

d_{\kappa}=\mathfrak{s}_{n}\otimes\left(\gamma_{(1)}\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}[(\kappa\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}})\Delta]\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{d}_{\kappa}=\overline{\mathfrak{s}}_{n}\otimes\left(\gamma_{(1)}\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}[(\kappa\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}})\Delta]\right).

By the definition of twisted composite products (Section
5.1
) and Remark
5.17
, the
derivations

d_{\kappa^{10}},d_{\kappa^{01}}:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}\to\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}

are given by

(\gamma_{(1)}\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}_{pp}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}[(\mathfrak{s}\otimes\kappa)\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}_{pp}})\Delta]\quad\text{and}\quad(\gamma_{(1)}\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}_{pp}})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}[(\overline{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\kappa)\circ\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}_{pp}})\Delta].

Then, the map
\varphi
is a map of bicomplexes if
\varphi d_{\kappa}=d_{\kappa^{10}}\varphi
and
\varphi\overline{d}_{\kappa}=d_{\kappa^{01}}\varphi
.

Let us check the first identity.
On the one hand, we have

\displaystyle\varphi d_{\kappa}\big((p,q)_{n}\otimes(\mu;\nu_{i_{1}},\dots,\nu_{i_{k}})\big)=

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum(-1)^{\theta+\sum_{s=1}^{j-1}d_{s}}(\iota\mathfrak{s}_{n}(p,q)_{n})\otimes(\mu\circ_{j}\kappa\nu_{i_{j}}^{(1)};\nu_{i_{1}},\dots,\nu_{i_{j}}^{(2)},\dots,\nu_{i_{k}}),

where
\nu_{i_{j}}^{(2)}=(\nu_{{i_{j}},1},\dots,\nu_{{i_{j}},\ell})
,
\theta
is the sign given by the decomposition map of
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
,
d_{s}=|\nu_{i_{s}}|
and

\iota=\iota_{d_{1},\dots,{d_{j}}_{1},\dots,{d_{j}}_{\ell},\dots,d_{k}}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{{d_{1}},\dots,d_{k}}\to\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{d_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{d_{k}},

defined in Section
5.2
.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle d_{\kappa^{10}}\varphi\big((p,q)_{n}\otimes(\mu_{n};\nu_{i_{1}},\dots,\nu_{i_{k}})\big)=

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(-1)^{\theta}\big((\mathrm{id}^{j-1}\otimes(\mathfrak{s}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\ell})\iota_{{d_{j}}_{0},\dots,{d_{j}}_{\ell}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{k-j})\iota_{d_{1},\dots,d_{k}}(p,q)_{n}\big)(\mu\circ_{j}\kappa\nu_{i_{j}}^{(1)};\nu_{i_{1}},\dots,\nu_{i_{j}}^{(2)},\dots,\nu_{i_{k}}),

where
d_{0}=|\nu_{i_{j}}^{(1)}|
and
\mathfrak{s}(p,q)_{n}=\mathfrak{s}_{1}
if
n=1
and zero otherwise.
By coassociativy of the maps
\iota
, Proposition
5.7
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes j-1}\otimes(\mathfrak{s}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes\ell})\iota_{{d_{j}}_{0},\dots,{d_{j}}_{\ell}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-j})\iota_{d_{1},\dots,d_{k}}(p,q)_{n}=

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes j-1}\otimes\mathfrak{s}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k+\ell-j})\iota_{d_{1},\dots,{d_{j}}_{0},\dots,{d_{j}}_{\ell},\dots,d_{k}}(p,q)_{n},

which by the compatibility relations between the maps
\iota
and
\mathfrak{s}
, Proposition
5.10
, gives

\displaystyle(-1)^{\sum_{s}^{j-1}d_{s}}\iota_{d_{1},\dots,{d_{j}}_{1},\dots,{d_{j}}_{\ell},\dots,d_{k}}\mathfrak{s_{n}}(p,q)_{n}.

This proves that
\varphi d_{\kappa}=d_{\kappa^{10}}\varphi
.
A similar computation shows
\varphi\overline{d}_{\kappa}=d_{\kappa^{01}}\varphi
.

This gives us a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Thus, by the two-out-of-three property, it suffices to show that
\varphi
is a pluripotential weak equivalence, which we address in the remainder of the proof.

Since
\mathcal{P}
is concentrated in bidegree
(0,0)
,
\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})
and
\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})
are
\mathrm{bb}\text{-}\mathbb{S}\,
-modules in bounded bicomplexes, see Remark
2.5
. Therefore, to show
\varphi
is a pluripotential weak equivalence is enough to show it is a quasi-isomorphism with respect to
\partial
and
{\bar{\partial}}
.
Let us focus on the
\partial
-case, so we forget the differential
{\bar{\partial}}
and view
\varphi
as a map of complexes with respect to the
\partial
-component on both sides. The strategy will actually be to split the component
\partial
into weights, obtaining a new bicomplex structure. Indeed, the natural weight-grading of
\mathcal{P}

induces a weight-grading on
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
and the differential
\partial

of
\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})

splits into two components of weight 0 and 1, respectively,
\partial=\partial_{0}+\partial_{1}
,
where
\partial_{1}=d_{\kappa}
. So we get that
\mathrm{Inf}(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}})
is a bicomplex with total differential
\partial
.
A similar argument shows that
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
with its
{\bar{\partial}}
-differential is also a bicomplex. Moreover,
\varphi
is a map of bicomplexes with respect to the weight-grading.
By Proposition
5.11
,
\varphi
is a quasi-isomorphism with respect to the weight 0 component
\partial_{0}
of
\partial
.
Therefore, a standard spectral sequence argument shows it is a quasi-isomorphism with respect to the total differential. This proves that
H_{\partial}(\varphi)
is an isomorphism.
A parallel argument shows
H_{\bar{\partial}}(\varphi)
is also an isomorphism.
∎

5.5.
Minimal models via Koszul duality

We present a concrete and computationally effective method for constructing pluripotential minimal models for operads in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbf{k}}
that are Koszul as a dg-operad, taking advantage of the dg setting. Fix
\mathcal{P}
a quadratic operad in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbf{k}}
.

A
coaugmented cooperad
is a cooperad
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a morphism

\eta:\mathrm{I}\to\mathcal{C}
of cooperads. Denote by
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
the coaugmentation coideal, that is
\overline{\mathcal{C}}:=\mathrm{Coker}(\eta)
.

Let
s
denote the
\mathbb{S}
-module in graded vector spaces defined by

(0,\,\mathbf{k}s,\,0,\,\dots),

where
\mathbf{k}s
is concentrated in degree
-1
.

Recall that the cobar construction of a coaugmented dg-cooperad
\mathcal{C}
(see, for instance, Section 6.5
[
19
]
) is given by

\Omega(\mathcal{C})=(\mathbb{T}(s\overline{\mathcal{C}}),d_{1}+d_{2})

where
s\mathcal{C}=s\otimes\mathcal{C}
,
d_{1}
is the derivation extending
d_{\mathcal{C}}
and
d_{2}
is the derivation extending

\delta:s\overline{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\Delta_{s}\otimes\Delta_{(1)}}s\otimes s\otimes\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{(1)}\overline{\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{\cong}s\overline{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{(1)}s\overline{\mathcal{C}}

where
\Delta_{s}(s)=-s\otimes s
.

We define a quasi-free operad in bicomplexes

\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}:=\left(\mathbb{T}(\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})),\partial_{1}+\partial_{2},{\bar{\partial}}_{1}+{\bar{\partial}}_{2}\right)

where
\partial_{1}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{1}
are the derivations extending the differentials of
\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})
.
The derivations
\partial_{2}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{2}
are defined as follows.
Recall from Section
5.2
the maps of bidegree
(1,0)

\mathfrak{s}_{n}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{n}\longrightarrow\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{n+1}

defined for
n\leq 0
.
Then
\partial_{2}
is the derivation extending the following map:

\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{s}\otimes\delta}\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}\circ_{(1)}s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})\xrightarrow{\psi_{s\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}},s\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}}}\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})\circ_{(1)}\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})

where
\mathfrak{s}
is induced by the maps
\mathfrak{s}_{n}
,
\delta
is the generating map for the derivation of the cobar construction given above, and
\psi_{s\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}},s\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}}
is the colax structure of the inflation functor, see Proposition
5.11
.

Explicitly, let
\mu
be an element in
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
and denote
\mu^{p,q}:=(p,q)_{|\mu|+1}\otimes s\mu\in\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})
. We have:

(\partial_{1}+\partial_{2})(\mu^{p,q})=q\mu^{p+1,q}+\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p+1\\
q_{1}+q_{1}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon+|\mu_{i}^{\prime}|}({\mu_{i}^{\prime}}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{e_{i}}{\mu_{i}^{\prime\prime}}^{p_{2},q_{2}})\sigma_{i}

where
\varepsilon=p+q+|\mu|+1+(p_{1}+q_{1})(p_{2}+q_{2}+|\mu_{i}^{\prime\prime}|+1)
. Similarly, we use the maps
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}
to construct
{\bar{\partial}}_{2}
as the derivation extending

\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes\delta}\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}\circ_{(1)}s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})\xrightarrow{\psi_{s\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}},s\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}}}\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})\circ_{(1)}\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}).

In this case, we have:

({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+{\bar{\partial}}_{2})(\mu^{p,q})=-p\mu^{p,q+1}+\sum_{i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{1}=q+1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon+|\mu_{i}^{\prime}|}({\mu_{i}^{\prime}}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{e_{i}}{\mu_{i}^{\prime\prime}}^{p_{2},q_{2}})\sigma_{i}.

Proposition 5.20
.

The tuple
(\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty},\partial,{\bar{\partial}})
satisfies the bicomplex equations

\partial^{2}={\bar{\partial}}^{2}=\partial{\bar{\partial}}+{\bar{\partial}}\partial=0.

Proof.

We will prove that
\partial^{2}=0
. The other two identities follow by analogous arguments.
Recall that the colax structure of the inflation functor involves the maps introduced in Section
5.2

\iota_{k_{1},\dots,k_{n}}\colon\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{k_{1}+\dots+k_{n}}\longrightarrow\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{k_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope

{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{k_{n}}.

For the computations below, we write

d_{i}=|s\mu_{i}|
.

\displaystyle\partial\partial((p,q)_{n}\otimes s\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum(-1)^{d_{1}+1}\left(\iota_{d_{1},d_{2}}\mathfrak{s}\partial_{\llcorner}(p,q)_{n}\right)\otimes s\mu_{(1)}\circ_{i}s\mu_{(2)}

\displaystyle+\sum(-1)^{d_{1}+1}\left(\partial_{\llcorner}\iota_{d_{1},d_{2}}\mathfrak{s}(p,q)_{n}\right)\otimes s\mu_{(1)}\circ_{i}s\mu_{(2)}

\displaystyle+\sum(-1)^{d_{2}+1}\left((\iota_{d_{1},d_{2}}\mathfrak{s}\otimes\mathrm{id})\iota_{d_{1}+d_{2}+1,d_{3}}\mathfrak{s}(p,q)_{n}\right)\otimes s\mu_{(1)}\circ_{i}s\mu_{(2)}\circ_{j}s\mu_{(3)}

\displaystyle+\sum(-1)^{d_{1}+d_{2}}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\iota_{d_{2},d_{3}}\mathfrak{s})\iota_{d_{1},d_{2}+d_{3}+1}\mathfrak{s}(p,q)_{n}\otimes s\mu_{(1)}\circ_{i}s\mu_{(2)}\circ_{j}s\mu_{(3)}.

The two first summands vanish because
\iota_{k,\ell}
are maps of bicomplexes and by Remark
5.9
. The two last summands vanish by the relations satisfied by
\iota_{k,\ell}
and
\mathfrak{s}
, Proposition
5.10
.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

There is a naive cobar construction for a coaugmented cooperad
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathrm{BiCo}_{\mathbf{k}}
using the shift
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to6.92pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 7.87 L 7.87 7.87}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.87 0 L 7.87 8.46}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}:\mathrm{BiCo}_{\mathbf{k}}\to\mathrm{BiCo}_{\mathbf{k}}
, defined in Section
2.1
, that mimics the cobar construction in the dg case. However, since the shift does not preserve minimality of bicomplexes, we will typically obtain an operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
which is not minimal as an operad in
\mathrm{BiCo}_{\mathbf{k}}
, see Definition
4.10
. The idea of the construction presented here is shifting before and then apply the inflation functor, so that minimality is ensured.

Theorem 5.22

(Minimal model of a Koszul operad)
.

The operad

\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is the pluripotential minimal model of
\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

We first show that
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is a minimal operad, see Section
4.2
.
We need to show that it is cofibrant and

\partial{\bar{\partial}}
decomposes, that is:

\partial{\bar{\partial}}(\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}))\subseteq(\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp})^{(\geq 2)}.

Note first that
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is cofibrant since it is the colimit of a sequence of principal extensions starting from the initial operad

\mathrm{I}\to\mathbb{T}(\mathrm{I}\oplus V_{2})\to\dots\to\mathbb{T}(\mathcal{P}_{n-1}\oplus V_{n})\to\dots\to\mathrm{colim}_{n}\mathcal{P}_{n}=\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}

where the extensions are given by

V_{2n}=H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}(\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}(n)))\text{ and }V_{2n+1}=H_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}(n))).

By construction,
\partial_{2}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{2}
decompose. Note that
\partial_{1}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{1}
do not decompose in general but in each arity,
\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})
is a minimal bicomplex, and so
\partial_{1}{\bar{\partial}}_{1}=0
. This shows
that
\partial{\bar{\partial}}
decomposes
and so the operad is minimal.

By writing
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}(E,R)
, define

\alpha\colon\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}\to\mathcal{P}

as the map extending

\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}})=\mathrm{Inf}(s\mathcal{C}(s^{-1}E,s^{-2}R))\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{Inf}(E)=E\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}.

It only remains to show that
\alpha
is a pluripotential weak equivalence. Since all bicomplexes are bounded, it suffices to show it induces isomorphisms in
H_{\partial}
and
H_{\bar{\partial}}
, see Remark
2.5
.
Note that, by considering
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
in bidegree
(*,0)
, we may view it as a sub-cooperad of
\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}
. Moreover, we have
H_{\partial}(\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}(n))=\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}(n)

so this inclusion of cooperads is an
H_{\partial}
-isomorphism.
This allows us to identify
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
as the suboperad of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}

generated by
H_{\partial}(\mathrm{Inf}(s\overline{\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}}))
. In particular, we have an inclusion
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
, which we claim is an
H_{\partial}
-isomorphism. Indeed,
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is weight-graded since it is a free operad. The differential
\partial
of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
splits into the two components
\partial=\partial_{1}+\partial_{2}
of weight 0 and 1, respectively. So we get that
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is a bicomplex with total differential
\partial
. In a similar way,
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
is a bicomplex with total differential
\partial=\partial_{2}
. Moreover, the inclusion

\mathcal{P}_{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is a map of bicomplexes with respect to the weight-grading and it is a quasi-isomorphism with respect to the weight 0 component. Therefore, a standard spectral sequence argument shows that
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
is an
H_{\partial}
-isomorphism.

The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}_{\infty}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

commutes and so
\alpha
is an
H_{\partial}
-isomorphism.
An analogous argument embedding
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}
in bidegree
(0,*)
gives that
\alpha
is an
H_{\bar{\partial}}
-isomorphism.
∎

Example 5.23
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=As
(as a non-symmetric operad), then
A^{pp}_{\infty}=\mathbb{T}(\mathrm{Inf}(sAs^{\mbox{!`}}))
and the differentials give

\partial\mu_{n}^{p,q}=q\mu_{n}^{p+1,q}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq i\leq k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p+1\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{i(\ell+1)+n+\varepsilon}\mu_{k}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{i}\mu_{\ell}^{p_{2},q_{2}}

and

{\bar{\partial}}\mu_{n}^{p,q}=-p\mu_{n}^{p,q+1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq i\leq k\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q+1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{i(\ell+1)+n+\varepsilon}\mu_{k}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{i}\mu_{\ell}^{p_{2},q_{2}},

where
\varepsilon=p+q+n+(p_{1}+q_{1})(p_{2}+q_{2}+\ell)
and we have set
\mu_{n}^{p,q}:=(p,q)_{2-n}\otimes s\mu_{n}
for
\mu_{n}\in\mathrm{As}^{\mbox{!`}}(n)
.
Algebras over this operad are precisely the pluripotential
A_{\infty}
-algebras introduced in Section
2.2
.

Example 5.24
.

If
\mathcal{P}=Lie
, we obtain
L_{\infty}^{pp}=\mathbb{T}(\mathrm{Inf}(sLie^{\mbox{!`}}))
and the differentials give

\partial\ell_{n}^{p,q}=q\ell_{n}^{p+1,q}+\sum_{r+s=n+1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}_{r,\,s}^{-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p+1\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(s+1)+\varepsilon}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)(\ell_{r}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{1}\ell_{s}^{p_{2},q_{2}})\sigma

and

{\bar{\partial}}\ell_{n}^{p,q}=-p\ell_{n}^{p,q+1}+\sum_{r+s=n+1}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}_{r,\,s}^{-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q+1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(s+1)+\varepsilon}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)(\ell_{r}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ_{1}\ell_{s}^{p_{2},q_{2}})\sigma,

where
\varepsilon=p+q+n+(p_{1}+q_{1})(p_{2}+q_{2}+s)
. We obtain thus, that a pluripotential
L_{\infty}
-algebra structure on a bicomplex
(L,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})
is a family of operations

\ell_{n}^{p,q}:L^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow L,\qquad n=2,3,\ldots,

of bidegrees
1-n\leq p+q\leq 2-n
, such that each
\ell_{n}^{p,q}
is anti-symmetric and such that

[\partial,\ell_{n}^{p,q}]=q\ell_{n}^{p+1,q}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+s=n+1\end{subarray}}\;\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}_{r,\,s}^{-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p+1\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q\end{subarray}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\,(-1)^{r(s+1)+\varepsilon}\,\ell_{r}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ\bigl(\ell_{s}^{p_{2},q_{2}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes(r-1)}\bigr)\sigma

and

[{\bar{\partial}},\ell_{n}^{p,q}]=-p\ell_{n}^{p,q+1}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r+s=n+1\end{subarray}}\;\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}_{r,\,s}^{-1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}=p\\
q_{1}+q_{2}=q+1\end{subarray}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\,(-1)^{r(s+1)+\varepsilon}\,\ell_{r}^{p_{1},q_{1}}\circ\bigl(\ell_{s}^{p_{2},q_{2}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes(r-1)}\bigr)\sigma

hold.

6.
Homotopy theory of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras

In this section we define the bar and cobar costructions for pluripotential
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and show that when
\mathcal{P}
is Koszul, the homotopy category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras is equivalent to the homotopy category of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}
-algebras with their
\infty
-morphisms.

6.1.
Bar and Cobar constructions for operadic algebras

Let
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{C}
be an operad and a cooperad in bicomplexes.
For the sake of simplicity, we will
restrict to
connected cooperads
, that is
\mathcal{C}(0)=0
and
\mathcal{C}(1)=\mathbf{k}
,
which is sufficient for our interests. This condition may be removed assuming conilpotency on coalgebras (see Section 5.8.4 in
[
19
]
).

Using the notion of twisting morphism

\varphi=(\varphi^{10},\varphi^{01}):\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}

introduced in Section
5.1
, we will
associate a
pair of adjoint functors

\Omega_{\varphi}:\mathcal{C}\text{-coalgebras}\rightleftarrows{\mathcal{P}\text{-algebras}:\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}}

relating algebras and coalgebras in bicomplexes.
The construction is analogous to the classical dg setting with the obvious difference introduced by the bicomplex structures. We will follow the construction as explained by Getzler and Jones in
[
16
]
.

Let
(A,\partial_{A},{\bar{\partial}}_{A},\gamma)
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Consider the cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
\mathcal{C}(A)
and the induced square zero coderivations

\partial_{1}:=\partial_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\mathrm{id}_{A}+\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}\partial_{A}\quad\text{and}\quad{\bar{\partial}}_{1}:={\bar{\partial}}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\mathrm{id}_{A}+\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ^{\prime}{\bar{\partial}}_{A}.

For any linear map
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
, consider the unique coderivation
d^{c}_{\alpha}
extending

\mathcal{C}(A)\xrightarrow{\alpha\circ\mathrm{id}}\mathcal{P}(A)\xrightarrow{\gamma}A

as explained in Section
3.4
.
It is explicitly given by

d^{c}_{\alpha}:=(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ(\mathrm{id}_{A};\gamma))([(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{(1)}\alpha)\Delta_{(1)}]\circ\mathrm{id}_{A}).

Note that, for any two linear maps
\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
, the following relation is satisfied

d^{c}_{\alpha}d^{c}_{\beta}=d^{c}_{\alpha\star\beta},

see for instance
[
16
]
.

Recall from Definition
5.2
, that an operadic twisting morphism
\varphi
is given by two linear maps
\varphi^{10}
and
\varphi^{01}
which we will use to construct a functor

\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}:\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\to\mathcal{C}\text{-Coalg}

called the
bar construction with respect to
\varphi
. It is given on objects by

\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}(A):=(\mathcal{C}(A),\partial_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{10}},{\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{01}}).

By definition,
B_{\varphi}(A)
is a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra with two additional coderivations.
The following proposition shows that these are indeed anti-commuting codifferentials.

Proposition 6.1
.

The bar construction
B_{\varphi}(A)
satisfies the bicomplex equations:

\begin{cases}(\partial_{1}+d_{\varphi^{10}})^{2}=0,\\
({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d_{\varphi^{01}})^{2}=0,\text{ and}\\
(\partial_{1}+d_{\varphi^{10}})({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d_{\varphi^{01}})+({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d_{\varphi^{01}})(\partial_{1}+d_{\varphi^{10}})=0.\end{cases}

Proof.

The proof is parallel to that of Proposition
5.4
.
First, note that, as in the classical case, for any linear map
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}

we have

\partial_{1}d^{c}_{\alpha}+d^{c}_{\alpha}\partial_{1}=d^{c}_{\partial\alpha+\alpha\partial}\text{ and }{\bar{\partial}}_{1}d^{c}_{\alpha}+d^{c}_{\alpha}{\bar{\partial}}_{1}=d^{c}_{{\bar{\partial}}\alpha+\alpha{\bar{\partial}}}.

Since
\varphi
is a twisting morphism, the following relations are satisfied

[\partial,\varphi^{10}]+\varphi^{10}\star\varphi^{10}=0,\quad[{\bar{\partial}},\varphi^{01}]+\varphi^{01}\star\varphi^{01}=0\quad\text{and}\quad[\partial,\varphi^{01}]+\varphi^{01}\star\varphi^{10}+[{\bar{\partial}},\varphi^{10}]+\varphi^{10}\star\varphi^{01}=0.

Together with the fact that
d^{c}_{\alpha}d^{c}_{\beta}=d^{c}_{\alpha\star\beta}
imply

(\partial_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{10}})^{2}=0\text{,}\quad({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{01}})^{2}=0,

and

\displaystyle(\partial_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{10}})({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{01}})+({\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{01}})(\partial_{1}+d^{c}_{\varphi^{10}})=

\displaystyle=d^{c}_{[\partial,\varphi^{01}]+\varphi^{01}\star\varphi^{10}}+d^{c}_{[{\bar{\partial}},\varphi^{10}]+\varphi^{10}\star\varphi^{01}}=

\displaystyle\,0.

∎

Let
(C,\Delta,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})
be a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra. Consider the free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\mathcal{P}(C)
and the induced square zero derivations

\partial_{1}:=\partial_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\mathrm{id}_{C}+\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}\partial_{C}\quad\text{and}\quad{\bar{\partial}}_{1}:={\bar{\partial}}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\mathrm{id}_{C}+\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}{\bar{\partial}}_{C}.

Let
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be a linear map and consider the unique derivation
d^{a}_{\phi}
extending the following linear map

C\xrightarrow{\Delta}\mathcal{C}(C)\xrightarrow{\alpha\circ\mathrm{id}}\mathcal{P}(C),

it is explicitly given by

d^{a}_{\alpha}:=(\gamma_{(1)}\circ\mathrm{id}_{C})(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ^{\prime}[(\alpha\circ\mathrm{id})\Delta]).

Again, for any two maps
\alpha,\beta:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
, the following relation is satisfied

d^{a}_{\alpha}d^{a}_{\beta}=d^{a}_{\alpha\star\beta}.

Let
\varphi:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic twisting morphism. We define the
cobar construction of
C
with respect to
\varphi
as

\Omega_{\varphi}(C):=(\mathcal{P}(C),-\partial_{1}-d^{a}_{\varphi^{10}},-{\bar{\partial}}_{1}-d^{a}_{\varphi^{01}}).

The following is proven analogously to
Proposition
6.1
.

Proposition 6.2
.

The cobar construction
\Omega_{\varphi}(C)
satisfies the bicomplex equations.

We will now show that the two functors constructed above form an adjunction. Let
\varphi:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be an operadic twisting morphism,
(C,\Delta,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})
a
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra and
(A,\gamma,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})
a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. We define two unary operations
\star_{\varphi}
and
\overline{\star}_{\varphi}
in
\underline{\mathrm{Hom}}(C,A)
of degree
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
, respectively:

\star_{\varphi}(\alpha):C\xrightarrow{\Delta}\mathcal{C}(C)\xrightarrow{\varphi^{10}\circ\alpha}\mathcal{P}(A)\xrightarrow{\gamma}A

and

\overline{\star}_{\varphi}(\alpha):C\xrightarrow{\Delta}\mathcal{C}(C)\xrightarrow{\varphi^{01}\circ\alpha}\mathcal{P}(A)\xrightarrow{\gamma}A.

A map
\alpha:C\to A
of degree
(0,0)
is a
twisting morphism with respect to
\varphi
if it satisfies the following two relations

[\partial,\alpha]+\star_{\varphi}(\alpha)=0\quad\text{and}\quad[{\bar{\partial}},\alpha]+\overline{\star}_{\varphi}(\alpha)=0.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\varphi:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{P}
be a twisting morphism. For every
\mathcal{P}
-algebra and every
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra there exist natural bijections

\mathrm{Hom}_{\text{$\mathcal{P}$-Alg}}(\Omega_{\varphi}C,A)\cong\mathrm{Tw}_{\varphi}(C,A)\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\text{$\mathcal{C}$-Coalg}}(C,\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}A).

Proof.

Since
\Omega_{\varphi}
is a free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, a map
\tilde{\alpha}:\Omega_{\varphi}(C)\to A
is uniquely determined by its restriction to the generators
\alpha:C\to A
. The fact that
\tilde{\alpha}
commutes with the differentials is equivalent to the commutativity of the following two diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C+\mathcal{P}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{C}+(\varphi^{10}\circ\mathrm{id})\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha+\gamma(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\alpha)}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C+\mathcal{P}(C)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\;,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\partial}}_{C}+(\varphi^{01}\circ\mathrm{id})\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\partial}}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha+\gamma(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\alpha)}

which is equivalent to the two equations

[\partial,\alpha]+\star_{\varphi}(\alpha)=0\quad\text{and}\quad[{\bar{\partial}},\alpha]+\overline{\star}_{\varphi}(\alpha)=0.

On the other hand, since
\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}(A)
is cofree, a map
\tilde{\alpha}:C\to\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}(A)
is characterized by its restriction to the cogenerators,
\alpha:C\to A
. Then,
\tilde{\alpha}
commutes with the differentials if and only if the following two diagrams commute

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\alpha)\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{A}+\gamma(\varphi^{10}\circ\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}_{\varphi}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\;,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ\alpha)\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\partial}}_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bar{\partial}}_{A}+\gamma(\varphi^{01}\circ\mathrm{id})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

which gives

[\partial,\alpha]+\star_{\varphi}(\alpha)=0\quad\text{and}\quad[{\bar{\partial}},\alpha]+\overline{\star}_{\varphi}(\alpha)=0.\qed

6.2.
Codifferential viewpoint and pluripotential
\infty
-morphisms

In analogy with the classical case, we can describe

\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebras in terms of codifferentials on a cofree coalgebra, where
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
denotes the pluripotential minimal model of a Koszul operad
\mathcal{P}
, see Section
5.5
. In this case, we will obtain two anti-commuting codifferentials.
This is done analogously to the classical case, by defining a convenient twisting morphism

\iota:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}

and then resourcing to the bar construction. Classically,
\iota
is just defined by suspension.
In our case, we will make use of the maps

\mathfrak{s}_{n}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}

\lxSVG@closescope

\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{n}\to\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{n+1}

of bidegree
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
, respectively, defined in Section
5.2
. Explicitly, we consider the equivariant maps

\iota^{10}\colon\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{s}\otimes s}\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}\quad\text{
and}\quad\iota^{01}\colon\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\xrightarrow{\overline{\mathfrak{s}}\otimes s}\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}

given by

(i,j)_{n}\otimes\mu\mapsto(-1)^{i+j+n}(i+1,j)_{n+1}\otimes s\mu

and

(i,j)_{n}\otimes\mu\mapsto(-1)^{i+j+n}(i,j+1)_{n+1}\otimes s\mu,

respectively.

Proposition 6.4
.

The pair
\iota:=(\iota^{10},\iota^{01})
defines a twisting morphism.

Proof.

This follows from the relations satisfied by
\mathfrak{s}
,
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}
, and the differentials, as described in Remark
5.9
, together with the relations involving
\mathfrak{s}
,
\overline{\mathfrak{s}}
, and the maps
\iota_{k,\ell}\colon\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{k+\ell}\to\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{k}\otimes\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
