{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\ell}
given in Proposition
5.10
.
∎

The bar construction given in Section
6.1
associated to the twisting morphism
\iota
gives a functor

\mathrm{B}_{\iota}:\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\to\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\text{-}\mathrm{Coalg}.

As a consequence, any
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebra can be understood as two codifferentials on the
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
-cofree coalgebra which anti-commute.
Moreover, this viewpoint naturally gives a definition of
\infty
-morphism in the pluripotential case:

Definition 6.5
.

Let
A
and
B
be two
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebras. A
pluripotential
\infty
-morphism
,

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism for short,
f:A\rightsquigarrow B
is a coalgebra morphism between the bar constructions

f:\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A)\to\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(B).

Remark 6.6
.

Any morphism of cofree coalgebras is characterized by its projection to the cogenerators. So a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebras
f:A\rightsquigarrow B
is equivalently given by a map

\bar{f}:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A)\to B

satisfying

(\delta_{1}^{B}+\delta_{\iota^{10}}^{B})f=\overline{f}(\partial_{1}^{A}+d_{\iota^{10}}^{A})\quad\text{and}\quad(\overline{\delta}_{1}^{B}+\delta_{\iota^{01}}^{B})f=\overline{f}({\bar{\partial}}_{1}^{A}+d_{\iota^{01}}^{A}),

where
\partial_{1}+d_{\iota^{10}}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d_{\iota^{01}}
are the codifferentials of the bar construction defined in Section
6.1
and
\delta_{1}^{B}+\delta_{\iota^{10}}^{B}
and
\overline{\delta}_{1}^{B}+\delta_{\iota^{01}}^{B}
the projection of such codifferentials to the cogenerators.

We call a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalence
if the arity 1 component, that is
f_{1}:A\to B
, is a pluripotential weak equivalence.

Example 6.7
.

Taking
\mathcal{P}=As
(as a non symmetric operad) we obtain that a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism
f:A\rightsquigarrow B
of
A^{pp}_{\infty}
-algebras is a family of maps

f_{n}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{n}\otimes A^{\otimes n}\to B,\quad\text{for }n\geq 1.

The map
f
has to satisfy

(\partial_{1}^{B}+d_{\iota^{10}}^{B})f=f(\partial_{1}^{A}+d_{\iota^{10}}^{A})\quad\text{and}\quad({\bar{\partial}}_{1}^{B}+d_{\iota^{01}}^{B})f=f({\bar{\partial}}_{1}^{A}+d_{\iota^{01}}^{A}).

Unraveling the equation we obtain the equations of a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism in Definition
2.21
of Section
2.2
.

Let
\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}
denote the category whose objects are
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebras and whose morphisms are given by

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphisms. The following is automatic.

Proposition 6.8
.

The functor
\mathrm{B}_{\iota}
extends to a fully faithful embedding
\mathrm{B}_{\iota}

\mathrm{B}_{\iota}:\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\to\{\text{quasi-free }\mathcal{P}_{pp}^{\mbox{!`}}\text{-}\mathrm{Coalg}\}.

6.3.
Equivalence of homotopy categories

Throughout this section,
\mathcal{P}
is a quadratic operad in

\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbf{k}}
that is Koszul as dg-operad.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
denote its pluripotential minimal model given in Section
5.5
.
Recall that
\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}
denotes the category of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebras with

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphisms, and
\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}
the category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras (in bicomplexes) with strict morphisms.
The main result of this section is the equivalence of homotopy categories

\mathrm{Ho}(\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg})\cong\mathrm{Ho}(\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}).

Consider the twisting morphism

\kappa:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\to\mathcal{P}

defined in Section
5.5
for (co)operads in bicomplexes.
By Proposition
6.3
, this gives an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\text{-Coalg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\text{-Alg}:\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}}}

between coalgebras and algebras in bicomplexes.
The counit of this adjunction is given as follows: for
A
a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra in bicomplexes, it is
the map of algebras extending

\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\circ\mathrm{id}_{A}}\mathrm{I}\circ A\cong A

where
\varepsilon
denotes the counit of the cooperad
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
. That is,

\varepsilon_{\kappa}:\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A=\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\mathrm{id}_{A}}\mathcal{P}(A)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}}A.

The unit is given by the map of coalgebras extending the map

C\cong\mathrm{I}\circ C\xrightarrow{\eta\circ\mathrm{id}_{C}}\mathcal{P}\circ C,

where
\eta
denotes the unit of the operad
\mathcal{P}
. That is,

C\xrightarrow{\Delta_{C}}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ C\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}^{\text{\textexclamdown}}_{pp}}\circ\eta\circ\mathrm{id}_{C}}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ\mathcal{P}\circ C=\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}\Omega_{\kappa}C.

We have

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a Koszul operad. For every
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
, the
counit

\varepsilon_{\kappa}:\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A\xrightarrow{\sim}A

is a pluripotential weak equivalence of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras.

Proof.

By definition, we have

\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A:=(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ A,\partial_{1}+d^{c}_{\kappa^{10}}+d^{a}_{\kappa^{10}},{\bar{\partial}}_{1}+d^{c}_{\kappa^{01}}+d^{a}_{\kappa^{01}}),

where
\partial_{1}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{1}
are the induced differentials of
A
on
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ A
,
d^{c}_{\kappa^{10}}
is the induced differential by that of the bar construction

\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A)\to\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A)\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\circ_{(1)}\kappa^{10})\circ\mathrm{id}_{A}}(\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{P})\circ A\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id};\gamma_{A})}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A),

and
d^{a}_{\kappa^{10}}
is the differential extending the following map

\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A)\to\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A)\xrightarrow{\kappa^{10}\circ\mathrm{id}\circ\mathrm{id}}\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}(A).

The differentials
d^{c}_{\kappa^{01}}
and
d^{a}_{\kappa^{01}}
are defined in the same manner but using
\kappa^{01}
. Since
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
are weight graded,
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A
is weight graded, and the weight is defined by the sum of the weights of
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
.
We define a bounded below and exhaustive increasing filtration

F_{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A):=\{\omega\mid\text{weight of }\omega\leq r\}.

The differentials of
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A
preserve the filtration. Indeed,
\partial_{1}
and
{\bar{\partial}}_{1}
are weight preserving as well as
d_{\kappa^{10}}^{a}
and
d_{\kappa^{01}}^{a}
. The differentials
d_{\kappa^{10}}^{c}
and
d_{\kappa^{01}}^{c}
are not weight preserving but decrease the weight, in particular we have

d^{c}_{\kappa^{**}}(F_{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A))\subseteq F_{r-1}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A).

These observations and the fact that
d^{a}_{\kappa^{10}}
and
d^{a}_{\kappa^{01}}
are constructed as the differentials of the twisted composite products (Section
5.1
) imply

\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}F_{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)/F_{r-1}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)=(\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp})(A).

Observe that, since
\mathcal{P}
is Koszul, by the ABC-Künneth formulae (Proposition
2.9
), and using the fact that
\mathbf{k}[\mathbb{S}_{n}]
is a semisimple ring, we obtain

H_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}\left((\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp})(A)\right)\cong\mathrm{I}(H_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}(A))\cong H_{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}(A).

This implies

H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}(F_{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)/F_{r-1}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A))=H_{\mathcal{A}}(F_{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)/F_{r-1}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A))=0

for
r>0
.

Endow
A
with the trivial filtration of weight 0.
Then, the counit of the adjunction

\varepsilon_{\kappa}:\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A\xrightarrow{\gamma_{A}(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\varepsilon\circ\mathrm{id}_{A})}A

is a filtered map. For
r=0
, we have

F_{0}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)=\mathrm{I}\circ\mathrm{I}\circ A\cong A\xrightarrow{\cong}A.

Assume inductively that, for all
0\leq s<r
, we have a pluripotential weak equivalence

F_{s}:=F_{s}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)\xrightarrow{\sim}A.

The map of short exact sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{r-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{r}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{r}/F_{r-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

induces a commutative diagram where the rows are exact (see Lemma
2.2
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{A}}^{p-1q-1}(F_{r-1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{A}}^{p-1q-1}(F_{r})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{A}}^{p-1q-1}(F_{r}/F_{r-1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}^{pq}(F_{r-1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}^{pq}(F_{r})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}^{pq}(F_{r}/F_{r-1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{A}}^{p-1q-1}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{A}}^{p-1q-1}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}^{pq}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\mathcal{B}\mathcal{C}}^{pq}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

By the observation above, we obtain
F_{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A)\xrightarrow{\sim}A.

Since the filtration is exhaustive, this implies
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A\xrightarrow{\sim}A.

∎

Consider the morphism

\varphi:\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P},

it induces the inclusion functor

\varphi^{*}:\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}.

Denote by

i:\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\hookrightarrow\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}

the obvious inclusion of categories.

Remark 6.10
.

Recall the twisting morphism

\iota:\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}

introduced in Section
6.1
. The twisting morphisms
\kappa
and
\iota
satisfy
\kappa=\varphi\,\iota.

From this identity, together with the definition of the bar construction (Section
6.1
), it follows that

\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}=\mathrm{B}_{\iota}\,i.

The following proposition is proved in exactly the same way as in the dg case, we include it here for completeness.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a Koszul operad. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}:\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}:i}}

Proof.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebra, consider its bar construction
\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A)
. We have the following morphism given by the unit of the adjunction associated to
\kappa

\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A\to\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}\Omega_{\kappa}(B_{\iota}A).

By the previous remark, we obtain a morphism

\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A\to\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(i\Omega_{\kappa}(B_{\iota}A)),

which gives a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism by Proposition
6.8

\eta(A):A\rightsquigarrow i\Omega_{\kappa}(\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A),

which induces a natural transformation
\eta:\mathrm{id}\to i\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}
.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, we have the following morphism of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras

\varepsilon_{\kappa}(A):\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}i(A)=\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}(A)\to A,

which induces a natural transformation of functors
\varepsilon\colon\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}i\to\mathrm{id}
.

Let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebra, the composite

\varepsilon(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota})\circ\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}\eta

is equal to

\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A)\xrightarrow{\Omega_{\kappa}(\eta_{\kappa}(B_{\iota}A))}\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{\kappa}(\Omega_{\kappa}(\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A))}\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A),

which is the identity by the adjunction associated to
\kappa
.
On the other hand, the composite
i(\varepsilon(A))\circ\eta(i(A))
is a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism, whose image under
\mathrm{B}_{\iota}
is given by

\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A\xrightarrow{\eta_{\kappa}(\mathrm{B}_{\kappa})}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A\xrightarrow{\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}(\varepsilon_{\kappa})}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}A,

which is the identity again by the adjunction associated to
\kappa
.
∎

Theorem 6.12

(Rectification)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a Koszul operad. There is a natural weak equivalence

\mathrm{id}_{\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}}\to\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}.

Proof.

To any
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{pp}
-algebra
A
, we apply the unit of the adjunction from Proposition
6.11
, namely

A\rightsquigarrow i\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A=\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A.

Recall that this

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism is defined by applying the unit of the adjunction associated to
\kappa
to
\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A
. To show that this map is a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalence, we need to prove that its first component is a pluripotential weak equivalence.

The first component is given by the composite:

A\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathrm{I}\circ\mathrm{I}\circ A\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ A.

We equip
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A
with a filtration by the total weight in
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}
. As in Proposition
6.9
, the associated graded object is

\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}F^{r}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A)/F^{r-1}(\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A)=\mathcal{P}\circ_{\kappa}\mathcal{P}^{\mbox{!`}}_{pp}\circ A.

By an argument analogous to that in Proposition
6.9
, we conclude that the morphism
A\rightsquigarrow\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}A
is indeed a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalence.
∎

Proposition 6.13
.

The rectification functor

\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}:\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}\to\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}

sends

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalences to pluripotential weak equivalences.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.12
, we have the following commutative diagram of

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

We can restrict the diagram to the first components of the

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphisms to obtain the following commutative diagram of maps of bicomplexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

so
f
is a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalence if and only if
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}(f)
is as well.
∎

Theorem 6.14

(Equivalence of homotopy categories)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a Koszul operad. We have an equivalence of homotopy categories

\mathrm{Ho}(\mathcal{P}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg})\cong\mathrm{Ho}(\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}).

Proof.

Consider the adjuction of Proposition
6.11

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}:\infty\text{\,-}\mathcal{P}^{pp}_{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Alg}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}\text{-alg}:i.}}

The functor
i
preserves weak equivalences by definition. The functor
\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}
also preserves weak equivalences by Proposition
6.13
. This implies they induce an adjunction at the level of homotopy categories.
Moreover, the counit of the adjunction is given by

\varepsilon_{\kappa}:\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\iota}i(A)=\Omega_{\kappa}\mathrm{B}_{\kappa}(A)\to A,

which is a pluripotential weak equivalence of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras
by Propostition
6.9
. And the unit of the adjunction is a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalence by Theorem
6.12
. This proves that the unit and the counit induce an isomorphism at the level of homotopy categories.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of Lemma
2.27

We prove the key lemma underlying the Homotopy Transfer Theorem for bidifferential bigraded algebras.
Please do not print, save the planet.

Recall from Section
2.3
the
\mathfrak{p}
-kernels

\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{p,q}:A^{\otimes n}\to A

defined for
n\geq 2
. We shall prove the following result.

Lemma
2.27
.

Let
(A,\partial,{\bar{\partial}},\mu)
be a bidifferential bigraded algebra.
Given a contraction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{11}}

define

(1)

\nu_{n}^{p,q}:=f\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{p,q}g^{\otimes n}

and

(2)

g_{n}^{p,q}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i-\alpha=p\\
j-\beta=q\end{subarray}}(-1)^{(\alpha+\beta)(i+j+n)}h_{\alpha\beta}\mathfrak{m}_{n}^{i,j}g^{\otimes n}

for all
n\geq 2
.

Then,
(B,\partial,{\bar{\partial}},\nu_{n}^{p,q})
is a
A^{pp}_{\infty}
-algebra, and

-weak equivalence

.

Lemma A.2
.

The
\mathfrak{p}
-kernels satisfy the following relations

(
\mathcal{M}_{n}
)

[\partial,\mathfrak{m}_{n}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\\
k,\ell\geq 2\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\mathfrak{m}_{k}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}){\Delta}^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}

and

(
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{n}
)

[{\bar{\partial}},\mathfrak{m}_{n}]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\\
k,\ell\geq 2\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\mathfrak{m}_{k}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r})\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}},r}_{k,\ell}

for every
n\geq 2
.

Proof.

We will only prove the relations

M
n

, the relations

¯
M

n

are analogous.
We have
\mathfrak{m}_{2}=\mu
and it satisfies the relation
(\mathcal{M}_{2})
. Assume inductively that
\mathfrak{m}_{m}
satisfies the equation
(\mathcal{M}_{m})
for all
m<n
. By definition,

\displaystyle[\partial,\mathfrak{m}_{n}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\partial\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}\partial,

where, by abuse of notation,
\partial
also denotes the induced differential on the tensor product. Using
\partial\mu=\mu\partial
and
\partial\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}=\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}\partial
yields

\displaystyle[\partial,\mathfrak{m}_{n}]=

(P1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\partial\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\partial\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P3)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\partial\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P4)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\partial\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}.

Applying the induction hypothesis

-\mathfrak{m}_{k}\partial=-\partial\mathfrak{m}_{k}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}=k+1\\
1\leq r\leq k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{{r}(1+{\ell^{\prime}})+{k}}\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{r}}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k^{\prime}-r})\Delta^{\partial,r}_{{k^{\prime}},{\ell^{\prime}}}

for
k\geq 2
to
P3
and
P4
gives

\displaystyle\text{\ref{P3}}=

(P3.1)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}\partial(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P3.2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}=k+1\\
1\leq r\leq k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)+r(1+\ell^{\prime})+k}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{r}},gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}},\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{k^{\prime}-r}})\Delta^{\partial,r}_{{k^{\prime}},{\ell^{\prime}}}),\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P3.3)

\displaystyle-(-1)^{n}\mu\big(\partial\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n-1})\big)\Delta^{h}_{1,n-1}

and

\displaystyle\text{\ref{P4}}=

(P4.1)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}\partial(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P4.2)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}=\ell+1\\
1\leq r\leq k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)+r(1+\ell^{\prime})+\ell}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{k}),\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{r}},gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}},\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{k^{\prime}-r}})\Delta^{\partial,r}_{{k^{\prime}},{\ell^{\prime}}})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

(P4.3)

\displaystyle-\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n-1})\otimes\partial\big)\Delta^{h}_{n-1,1}.

Now grouping
P1
+
P3.1
+
P3.3
and
P2
+
P4.1
+
P4.3
leads to

\displaystyle\text{\ref{P1}+\ref{P3.1}+\ref{P3.3}+\ref{P2}+\ref{P4.1}+\ref{P4.3}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big((\partial\bm{h}-\bm{h}\partial)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
l>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes(\partial\bm{h}-\bm{h}\partial)(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}.

From Remark
A.1
, the identity
[\partial,\bm{h}]=(gf-\mathrm{id})(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})
implies

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(gf(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big((\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes gf(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}.

Expanding the definitions of
\mathfrak{m}_{k}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}
, we obtain

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big((\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}+\ell=n\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{1}}\mu\Big(\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}}),\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}})\big),\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\Big)(\mathfrak{s}_{1},\Delta^{h}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}},\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 2+\ell})\Delta^{h}_{k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime},\ell}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}=n\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{2}}\mu\Big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{k}),\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}}),\bm{h}(\mathrm{id},\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}})\big)\Big)(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 2+k},\mathfrak{s}_{1},\Delta^{h}_{k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}})\Delta^{h}_{k,k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}}=0,

where
\varepsilon_{1}=(k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime})(\ell+1)+k^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}+1)
and
\varepsilon_{2}=k(k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}+1)+k^{\prime}(\ell^{\prime}+1)
. Here, we have employed the associativity of
\mu
and the relation

(\mathfrak{s}_{1},\Delta^{h}_{k_{1},k_{2}},\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 2+k_{3}})\Delta^{h}_{k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3}}=(-1)^{k_{1}+1}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes 2+k_{1}},\mathfrak{s}_{1},\Delta^{h}_{k_{2},k_{3}})\Delta^{h}_{k_{1},k_{2}+k_{3}}.

We rewrite

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(gf(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes gf(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

as

(P5)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
k>1\end{subarray}}\mu\big(\mathrm{id}\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{1,\ell}(\mathrm{id}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{k}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes\ell})\Delta^{\partial,1}_{\ell+1,k}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n\\
\ell>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)+k+1}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\mathrm{id})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,1}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k+1}\otimes ip\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\Delta^{\partial,k+1}_{k+1,\ell}.

The expression
P3.2
becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{1}}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k^{\prime},\ell}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{r}}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{k^{\prime}-r+\ell}})\Delta^{\partial,r}_{{k^{\prime}+\ell},{\ell^{\prime}}},

where
\varepsilon_{1}=r(1+\ell^{\prime})+k^{\prime}+\ell+1+(k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}+1)(\ell+1)+\ell^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1
.

Similarly, we rewrite
P4.2
as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime},\ell^{\prime}>1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\varepsilon_{2}}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k^{\prime}})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,k^{\prime}}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{k+r}}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell^{\prime}}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes{k^{\prime}-r}})\Delta^{\partial,k+r}_{{k+k^{\prime}},{\ell^{\prime}}},

where
\varepsilon_{2}=k(k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime})+r(1+\ell^{\prime})+k^{\prime}+\ell^{\prime}+k+1
.
Finally, reindexing and summing
P5
+
P3.2
+
P4.2
completes the proof.
∎

Remark A.3
.

A pluripotential
A_{\infty}
-algebra is equivalently defined as a bicomplex
(A,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})
together with
n
-ary operations

\bm{\mu}_{n}\colon\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{n-2}\otimes A^{\otimes n}\to A

for each
n\geq 2
, each of bidegree
(0,0)
, satisfying the following conditions:

\displaystyle[\partial,\bm{\mu}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,\bm{\mu}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\mu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell},

\displaystyle[{\bar{\partial}},\bm{\mu}_{n}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,\bm{\mu}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\mu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}},r}_{k,\ell}.

Lemma A.4
.

The
\mathfrak{p}
-kernels induce a
A^{pp}_{\infty}
-structure on
B
by the formula

\bm{\nu}_{n}=f\circ\mathfrak{m}_{n}\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n}).

Proof.

By Remark
A.3
, the operations
\bm{\nu}_{n}
constitute an
A^{pp}_{\infty}
-structure if and only if

\displaystyle[\partial,\bm{\nu}_{n}]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,\bm{\nu}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\nu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}=0,

and

\displaystyle[{\bar{\partial}},\bm{\nu}_{n}]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,\bm{\nu}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\nu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}},r}_{k,\ell}=0.

Expanding the definition of
\bm{\nu}_{n}
in the first expression, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial f\mathfrak{m}_{n}(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n})-f\mathfrak{m}_{n}(\partial\otimes g^{\otimes n})-\sum_{j=1}^{n}f\mathfrak{m}_{n}(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes j-1}\otimes g\partial\otimes g^{n-j})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,f\mathfrak{m}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes r-1}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes\ell})\otimes g^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}

which gives

\displaystyle f\bigg(\partial\mathfrak{m}_{n}-\sum_{j=1}^{n+1}\mathfrak{m}_{n}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes j-1}\otimes\partial\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes n+1-j})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\\
k,\ell\geq 2\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,\mathfrak{m}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}\bigg)(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle=0

by Lemma
A.2
. Here, we use that
g
and
f
are maps of bicomplexes and that

(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes\ell+s})\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}=\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n}).

A similar proof shows

\displaystyle[{\bar{\partial}},\bm{\nu}_{n}]-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\,\bm{\nu}_{k}\left(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\nu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}\right)\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}},r}_{k,\ell}=0.

∎

Remark A.5
.

Let
(A,\bm{\mu}_{*}^{A})
and
(B,\bm{\mu}_{*}^{B})
be two pluripotential
A_{\infty}
-algebras. Similarly to Remark
A.3
, a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism
f\colon A\rightsquigarrow B
is equivalently defined as a family of bidegree-preserving maps

\bm{f}_{n}\colon\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

which satisfy the following relations:

\displaystyle[{\bar{\partial}},\bm{f}_{n}]=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\

and

\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}}}_{k,i_{1}+1,\dots,i_{k}+1}:=(\tau_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}})(\iota_{2\text{-}k,1\text{-}i_{1},\dots,1\text{-}i_{k}})\overline{\mathfrak{s}}_{1\text{-}n}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope

}

where
\tau_{i_{1},\dots,i_{k}}
is the isomorphism that permutes the tensor factors.
The sign
\theta
is given by

\theta(i_{1},\dots,i_{k}):=\sum_{1\leq r<s\leq k}i_{r}(i_{s}+1).

Lemma A.6
.

The
\mathfrak{p}
-kernels define a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-morphism
g:B\rightsquigarrow A
via the formula

\bm{g}_{n}:=\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n}),

for
n\geq 2
and
\bm{g}_{1}=g
.

Proof.

Let us compute

\partial\bm{g}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n}\partial=\partial\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})-\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})\partial.

By Lemma
A.2

\displaystyle\partial\bm{g}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n}\partial=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\;\partial\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})-\bm{h}\partial(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\\
k,\ell\geq 2\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\theta}\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r},gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell},\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}))(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n}),

with
\theta=r(1+\ell)+n.

Now we use the relation
[\partial,\bm{h}]=(gf-\mathrm{id})(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathrm{id})
in Remark
A.1
and that

(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta^{\partial,r}_{k,\ell})\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}=-(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}k}\otimes\mathrm{id})\Delta_{k+1,\ell}^{\partial,r}

to obtain

\displaystyle gf(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})-(\mathfrak{s}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\\
k,\ell\geq 2\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}k}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k})(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes gf\mathfrak{m}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r}))\Delta_{k+1,\ell}^{\partial,r}(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n})

which gives

\displaystyle gf(\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n})\Delta^{\partial,1}_{2,n}-\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\,\mu\Bigl(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}\Bigr)\Delta^{\partial,1}_{2,n}\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\\
k,\ell\geq 2\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\bm{g}_{k}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\nu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r})\Delta^{\partial}_{k+1,\ell}=

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\,\mu\Bigl(\bm{g}_{k}\otimes\bm{g}_{\ell}\Bigr)\Delta^{\partial}_{2,k+1,\ell+1}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\bm{g}_{k}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\nu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r})\Delta^{\partial}_{k+1,\ell,r}.

A similar computation shows

\displaystyle{\bar{\partial}}\bm{g}_{n}-\bm{g}_{n}{\bar{\partial}}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\,\mu\Bigl(\bm{g}_{k}\otimes\bm{g}_{\ell}\Bigr)\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}}}_{2,k+1,\ell+1}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k+\ell=n+1\\
1\leq r\leq k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{r(1+\ell)+n}\bm{g}_{k}(\mathrm{id}^{\otimes r}\otimes\bm{\nu}_{\ell}\otimes\mathrm{id}^{\otimes k-r})\Delta^{{\bar{\partial}}}_{k+1,\ell,r}.\qed

Lemma
A.4
and Lemma
A.6
imply the following, which is equivalent to Lemma
2.27
by Remarks
A.3
and
A.5
.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
(A,\partial,{\bar{\partial}},\mu)
be a bidifferential bigraded algebra and let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,\partial,{\bar{\partial}},\mu)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(B,\partial,{\bar{\partial}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}

be a contraction. The
\mathfrak{p}
-kernels

\mathfrak{m}_{n}:\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-0.4pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0.59 L 7.87 0.59}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
{}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 0 8.66}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{2-n}\otimes A^{\otimes n}\to A

given by

\mathfrak{m}_{n}:=\sum_{k+\ell=n}(-1)^{k(\ell+1)}\mu\big(\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{k})\otimes\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{\ell})\big)\Delta^{h}_{k,\ell}

for
n\geq 2
induce

(1)

a
A^{pp}_{\infty}
-structure on
B
via
\bm{\nu}_{n}=f\circ\mathfrak{m}_{n}\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes g^{\otimes n})
, and

(2)

a

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pp}}{{\infty}}

-weak equivalence
g:B\rightsquigarrow A
via

\bm{g}_{n}:=\bm{h}(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{n})(\iota_{\text{-}1,2\text{-}n}\otimes g^{\otimes n})
for
n\geq 2
and
\bm{g}_{1}=g.
